### Message

For tilt grain boundaries (GBs) in FCC Ni with a <110> tilt axis, which Sigma GBs have a low-index plane, e.g., {100}, {110}, {111}, on one side of the GB? List all the candidates for sigma<=50, including GB plane pair information and provide a brief summary.

## 0001 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T20:10:44+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

The CSL enumeration identifies **nine nontrivial Σ values for FCC Ni with a ⟨110⟩ tilt axis and Σ ≤ 50**:

**Σ3, Σ9, Σ11, Σ17, Σ19, Σ27, Σ33, Σ41, and Σ43.**

Σ33 has **two distinct rotation branches**, so Σ alone does not uniquely specify the misorientation.

**Completeness caveat:** The available plane-pair search returned the 21 qualifying pairs below, but its bounded search **does not certify an exhaustive plane-pair list**. In particular, it returned no qualifying Σ41 pair. That is a search limitation—not evidence that Σ41 cannot have a low-index plane on one side.

### Low-index plane pairs returned

A concrete **[110]** axis is used in both grain-local coordinate systems. Other members of ⟨110⟩ give equivalent index representations under consistent cubic symmetry. Parentheses denote concrete planes; braces denote families.

The remaining rotation branch is **Σ41, \(m/n=8/3\), 55.877°**; its qualifying low-index plane pairs remain unresolved by this search.

### Brief summary

- Requiring a low-index plane on **only one side** admits many **asymmetric tilt GBs**, often with a high-index partner plane.
- The first Σ3 row is the **symmetric {111}/{111} tilt boundary**; the other returned rows are classified as asymmetric tilt.
- Multiple plane pairs can share the same Σ and rotation angle. Likewise, **Σ33 has two different angles**, which must be kept separate.
- GB plane 1/2 are crystallographic labels, **not spatial top/bottom assignments**. Σ1—the trivial identity case—is excluded.
- These are geometric CSL candidates, **not an energy or stability ranking**.

The search used its maximum supported plane-search limit of 6; generated partner indices can exceed that limit. A target-plane-seeded exact enumeration is still needed to certify **all** pairs, including the missing families in some branches.

## 0004 · User

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T20:19:37+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Message

I am trying to study general tilt GBs in FCC Ni, with tilt axis <110>. Please build and relax an FCC Ni {110} || {111} grain boundary. Determine compatible grain orientations and a common tilt axis, then choose suitable initial sizes and an available Ni potential to run the initial minimization. After minimization, render the strucure in both the top and plan view, then analyze possible structural periods.

## 0005 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T20:19:37+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

## Built structure and initial relaxation

I built the **general asymmetric {110} ∥ {111} pure-tilt GB in FCC Ni**, with an actual misorientation of **35.264390°**. No nearby CSL geometry was substituted and no Σ was assigned.

The family constraints resolve to one physically distinct geometry. The grain-local orientations below share the same physical tilt line, although their local Miller indices differ.

| Grain | GB plane / local y | Local x | Local tilt direction z | Repeat counts | Initial material dimensions, x × y × z |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Top | (110), [110] | [001] | [−1,1,0] | **[57,21,3]** | **20.064 × 10.454 × 1.493 nm** |
| Bottom | (111), [111] | [1,−2,1] | [−1,0,1] | **[23,17,3]** | **19.831 × 10.365 × 1.493 nm** |

The denser FCC **(111) plane was placed at the bottom**. The simulation frame is y = GB normal, z = tilt line, and x = y × z.

- **Potential:** registered **NiCr.adp**, using Ni with the ADP model; reference lattice parameter **3.52 Å**.
- **Atoms:** 56,880.
- **Initial x-length mismatch:** **1.16094%**, below 2%.
- **Boundary setup:** x direction with vacuum (non-contiguous periodic connection), with **2 nm vacuum per x side**.
- **Estimated initial padded box:** **24.064 × 24.818 × 1.495 nm**.

**Convergence warning:** The minimization completed and saved the structure, but its **final stage did not meet the requested tolerance**. It stopped because the line-search step became zero. The final force two-norm was **1.4914 × 10⁻⁴ eV/Å**, versus the requested **10⁻⁶ eV/Å**. The images and period analysis therefore characterize this saved, initially relaxed structure—not a demonstrated fully converged equilibrium GB.

## Structure views

### Top view — XY, viewed along z

CNA coloring, with all atoms visible.

![FCC Ni GB top view](artifacts/analysis/render/render-20261021-202310-9aa980/render.png)

### GB plan view — XZ, viewed along y

X points right and Z down. **Black denotes the lower-y facing layer; cream denotes the upper-y layer**, rather than CNA classifications. The selected facing-layer slab contains **900 atoms**; boundary and distorted-edge layer assignments are approximate.

![FCC Ni GB plan view](artifacts/analysis/render/render-20261021-202310-0f129c/plan_view.png)

## Possible structural periods

![Joint structural and elastic-strain periodicity summary](artifacts/analysis/gb_periodicity/gb-periodicity-20261021-202310-f3831c/multiscale_review/multiscale_summary.png)

The joint coordinate, atomic-row, and elastic-strain analysis gives **P012 as the highest-priority candidate for review**, not a uniquely established period or an automatically selected periodic cell.

| Candidate | Coordinate recurrence | Nominal top / bottom translations | Construction x mismatch | Main evidence |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| **P012 — review priority** | **3.8744 nm** | **11a[001] / 4.5a[1,−2,1]** | **0.206%** | Strong agreement among rows, coordinates, and local strain; about 4.60 observable cycles |
| P006 | 1.7433 nm | 5a[001] / 2a[1,−2,1] | 2.020% | Strong row support; weaker strain support |
| P007 | 2.1310 nm | 6a[001] / 2.5a[1,−2,1] | 2.020% | Strong row support; no local-strain support |
| P024 | 7.7496 nm | 22a[001] / 9a[1,−2,1] | 0.206% | Strong coordinate recurrence, but only about 2.30 cycles and no row/strain support |
| P005 | 1.3561 nm | 4a[001] / 1.5a[1,−2,1] | 8.144% | Weak structural support and large mismatch |

**Short motif:** P012 has a measured atomic-row motif of **3.8803 nm**, close to—but distinct from—its **3.8744 nm coordinate recurrence**.

**Longer modulation:** No longer structural-modulation candidate was established. P024 remains a coordinate-recurrence alternative, not confirmed long-wave modulation. The finite x extent cannot exclude a longer scale.

### Read-only size comparisons for a later continuous-periodic x cell

These retain the original y/z repeats. Dimensions are **nominal material dimensions**, not measurements of the relaxed structure.

| Candidate | Top / bottom repeat triplets | Top dimensions, nm | Bottom dimensions, nm |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P012** | **[11,21,3] / [4.5,17,3]** | **3.872 × 10.454 × 1.493** | **3.880 × 10.365 × 1.493** |
| P006 | [5,21,3] / [2,17,3] | 1.760 × 10.454 × 1.493 | 1.724 × 10.365 × 1.493 |
| P007 | [6,21,3] / [2.5,17,3] | 2.112 × 10.454 × 1.493 | 2.156 × 10.365 × 1.493 |
| P024 | [22,21,3] / [9,17,3] | 7.744 × 10.454 × 1.493 | 7.760 × 10.365 × 1.493 |
| P005 | [4,21,3] / [1.5,17,3] | 1.408 × 10.454 × 1.493 | 1.293 × 10.365 × 1.493 |

Only **P012 and P024** meet the strict construction mismatch limit of **<2%** among these options. All five pass the necessary pure-x lattice-repeat check; the fractional bottom coefficients are permitted for FCC [1,−2,1], but **a seamless connection of the relaxed structure still requires inspection**. Construction mismatch uses nominal lengths and a maximum-length denominator; it is distinct from the relaxed-pair mismatch metric.

**No periodic cell was selected, rebuilt, or strained.**

Representative ordered planes are **GB1 (110) || GB2 (111)**. The actual tilt misorientation is **35.26438968°**; this is a **general asymmetric pure-tilt boundary**. Each integer direction is reduced; \\(x=y\\times z\\), with directions normalized independently when constructing the rotation.\n\n| Spatial grain | Original side | Concrete local plane / y | Signed local tilt / z | Local x |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **Top** | GB1 | (110), [110] | [−1 1 0] | [001] |\n| **Bottom** | GB2 | (111), [111] | [−1 0 1] | [1 −2 1] |\n\nThese **different local z triples represent the same physical simulation tilt direction** after grain orientation. Do not substitute the raw cross product of the two grain-local normals for that physical axis.\n\n**Density assignment:** FCC {111} is denser than FCC {110}: their ideal planar densities are respectively \\(4/(\\sqrt3\\,a_0^2)\\) and \\(\\sqrt2/a_0^2\\). Thus **(111) goes to bottom**, consistent with the default; the ordered GB1/GB2 labels remain unchanged.\n\n**Evidence and downstream limits**\n- Family-resolution tool: `ambiguous=false`, `equivalence_class_count=1`, `raw_candidate_count=12`.\n- Concrete grain-local resolution: `ambiguous=false`; six signed axis/symmetry representatives, all at **35.26438968°**, rather than additional physical choices.\n- The frames above are obtained from the tool-resolved local normals/axes and their exact integer cross products. No exact-CSL orientation or period record was obtained; this general boundary must not be replaced by a nearby CSL construction.\n- LAMMPS must determine actual-basis translation periods, repeat counts, and whether compatible periodic dimensions require accommodation. This crystallography stage does **not** complete sizing, minimization, rendering, or periodicity analysis.&quot;, &quot;evidence_refs&quot;: [{&quot;kind&quot;: &quot;run&quot;, &quot;id&quot;: &quot;csl-20261021-201945-0edf88&quot;}], &quot;next_action&quot;: &quot;&quot;}}</pre>

## 0007 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T20:27:42+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

Created the **P012 FCC Ni (110) ∥ (111) structure with continuous periodic x**, along the GB direction, and no x-side vacuum. Your selected repeat counts—including the fractional bottom count **4.5**—were preserved exactly.

| Grain | Repeat counts | Nominal material dimensions, x × y × z |
|---|---|---|
| Top (110) | **[11,21,3]** | **3.872 × 10.454 × 1.493 nm** |
| Bottom (111) | **[4.5,17,3]** | **3.880 × 10.365 × 1.493 nm** |

The structure retains the **35.264390° general tilt geometry**, uses **NiCr.adp**, and contains **11,052 atoms**. Nominal construction x mismatch is **0.206%**.

### Minimization outcome

The minimization completed, but **the final stage did not reach the requested force tolerance**: it stopped at **1,000 iterations**, with a final force two-norm of **2.507 × 10⁻⁵ eV/Å**. The saved result is available, but should not yet be treated as a fully converged equilibrium structure.

### Top view — XY, CNA coloring

All atoms are visible.

![P012 periodic FCC Ni GB top view](artifacts/analysis/render/render-20261021-202943-baa730/render.png)

### GB plan view — XZ

X points right and Z down. Black and cream distinguish the lower- and upper-y facing layers; they are not CNA colors. The selected slab contains **174 atoms**.

![P012 periodic FCC Ni GB plan view](artifacts/analysis/render/render-20261021-202943-06cd69/plan_view.png)

The nominal FCC half-integer repeat is lattice-compatible, but **the images alone do not establish seamless relaxed periodic continuity**.

## 0009 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T20:31:24+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

The **70-step monotonic +x shear simulation completed**, using your strain increment of **2.5 × 10⁻⁴**. The final actual shear strain is **0.0175 (1.75%)**. All **71 recorded points**, including step 0, converged during shear relaxation.

## Stress–strain curve

The plot shows **top shear-stress magnitude versus actual shear strain**. No points were filtered or smoothed.

![P012 FCC Ni stress–strain curve](artifacts/analysis/stress_strain/stress-strain-20261021-203549-3d92dc/stress_strain_comparison.png)

## Main findings

| Feature | Step | Shear strain | Stress |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Maximum stress | **34** | **0.00850 (0.850%)** | **788.657 MPa** |
| Minimum following the main collapse | **37** | **0.00925 (0.925%)** | **−2.511 MPa signed** |
| Final state | **70** | **0.01750 (1.750%)** | **+21.759 MPa signed** |

- **Initial buildup and major collapse:** Stress rises to the peak at step 34, then collapses. The peak-to-minimum **magnitude drop is 786.146 MPa**. The largest single-step drop is **769.432 MPa**, at step 35.
- **Subsequent small fluctuations:** Eight additional local peak-to-valley events were detected. Their magnitude drops range from **11.60–20.85 MPa**, with a median of **18.88 MPa**. Later peak magnitudes are approximately **18–22 MPa**.
- **Recurrence:** Later peaks recur roughly every **4 loading steps**, corresponding to a strain interval of **0.001**.
- **Detection significance:** Only the main collapse exceeds the robust automatic drop threshold of **64.89 MPa**. The eight later events are local-extrema features, not comparably large stress drops.

Because the displayed curve uses absolute stress, it hides small **stress-sign reversals** after the collapse; the signed values remain available in the saved data.

### Interpretation and limitations

The response shows a **high-stress buildup followed by abrupt stress release and a much lower-stress fluctuating regime**. This is consistent with a substantial rearrangement or slip event, but the curve alone cannot identify **GB sliding, migration, or dislocation activity**. Comparing the exact saved structures around **steps 34–37** would be the most informative next check.

No elastic-modulus fit was performed. Also, although every shear point converged, the accepted starting structure’s final minimization had not reached its force tolerance.

## 0010 · User

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T21:19:07+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Message

In principle, the grain boundary sliding resistance should be a constant value; however, a massive stress drop occurred in our current loading mode, which is a phenomenon that theoretically should not happen. One possible reason is that the grain boundary structure underwent a permanent change during the first load drop. One verification method involves performing a reverse loading process in 70 equal steps to unload the strain back to zero and then reload to the original maximum displacement and observe whether a load drop of similar magnitude occurs during the reloading process.

---

## 0011 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T21:19:07+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

**The verification cycle completed, and no comparably large stress drop occurred during reloading.** This supports a history-dependent response, consistent with your structural-change hypothesis, but does not by itself prove permanent GB restructuring.

### Loading protocol verified

Starting from the previous shear endpoint, I applied:

1. **70 reverse-loading steps** of \(2.5\times10^{-4}\), returning cumulative strain from **0.0175 to zero**.
2. **70 forward-loading steps** of the same increment, returning to **0.0175**.

The final boundary displacement was restored exactly to the original maximum.

### Signed stress–strain comparison

![First loading versus unloading and reloading](artifacts/analysis/stress_strain/stress-strain-20261021-212925-c51b01/stress_strain_comparison.png)

The plot distinguishes **first loading** from the **chronological unloading/reloading continuation**; unloading and reloading share one legend entry.

### First loading versus reloading

The drop quantities below use **stress magnitude**, while the plot uses **signed stress**.

| Quantity | First loading | Reloading |
|---|---:|---:|
| Largest confirmed interior stress-magnitude peak | **788.657 MPa** at strain **0.00850** | **20.956 MPa** at strain **0.00600** |
| Largest confirmed peak-to-valley magnitude drop | **786.146 MPa** | **19.580 MPa** |
| Strain interval of that drop | 0.00850 → 0.00925 | 0.01550 → 0.01600 |
| Largest adjacent magnitude decrease within detected descents | **769.432 MPa** | **13.116 MPa** |

The largest reloading drop is **about 40 times smaller** than the original collapse. Reloading instead exhibits small recurring fluctuations, with subsequent drop magnitudes of **10.90–19.58 MPa**, similar to the low-stress fluctuations after the first collapse.

At the unloaded, zero-strain state, the residual signed top stress was **+12.105 MPa**. At the restored maximum displacement, it was **+20.438 MPa**, compared with **+21.759 MPa** at the original endpoint.

### Interpretation

The original high-resistance state **did not recover after unloading to zero**. This is consistent with the first collapse moving the system into a different metastable structural or sliding state.

However, constant steady sliding resistance does **not necessarily imply constant instantaneous stress** under incremental displacement-controlled relaxation: metastability and stick–slip-like rearrangements can produce stress buildup and drops. Thus, the initial collapse is not automatically unphysical.

To test **structural permanence directly**, the next useful comparison is between the original pre-loading structure and the unloaded zero-strain structure, together with frames immediately before and after the first collapse.

**Caveats:** Two continuation points were nonconverged; all finite points were retained. The turnaround and final states converged. The starting-structure minimization caveat also remains.

**Saved verification run:** `shear-20261021-211934-d84742`

## 0013 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T21:37:20+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

I compared the **initial zero-strain configuration** with the **unloaded zero-strain configuration**—not the final reloaded state. Atoms were matched by their unique IDs, with displacement vectors pointing **from initial to unloaded positions**.

### GB plan view with displacement arrows

![Zero-strain GB displacement comparison](artifacts/analysis/render/render-20261021-213748-f27cfe/plan_view.png)

- **View:** XZ plane, with X right and Z down.
- **Black/cream atoms:** lower-/upper-y facing layers of the unloaded structure.
- **Red arrows:** XZ-projected atomic displacements, shown at **physical scale without amplification**. The 2 Å vector cap was not reached.
- The displayed GB slab contains **174 atoms**.

### Measured differences

| Selection | Median displacement magnitude | Maximum displacement magnitude |
|---|---:|---:|
| Full structure — 11,052 atoms | **0.2971 Å** | **0.7805 Å** |
| Displayed GB slab — 174 atoms | **0.3044 Å** | **0.7805 Å** |

For the displayed slab, the maximum **XZ-projected** displacement is **0.7721 Å**.

**The unloaded structure has not returned atom-by-atom to its initial coordinates**, despite returning to zero imposed strain. Its residual top shear stress is **12.105 MPa**, compared with approximately **−0.00149 MPa** initially.

These differences support a persistent change over this loading–unloading cycle. However, they include **rigid translation and affine deformation**, so they do not yet isolate local GB rearrangement or prove permanent structural transformation. Minimum-image displacements were used; both selected frames were recorded as converged.

**Compared frames**
- Initial: `shear-20261021-203147-9d18c4`, step **0**
- Unloaded: `shear-20261021-211934-d84742`, local step **70**

### Message

However, the issue is that we only performed loading and unloading along x direction, without actively introducing rigid-body relative displacement; therefore, what you refer to as atomic rigid-body displacement should be considered part of the atomic structural changes. Thus, the simplest approach is to conduct an NEB calculation and examine the Minimum Energy Path (MEP) to determine the intermediate process.

## 0015 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T21:39:51+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

Agreed: the collective displacement emerged during loading and unloading, rather than being an independently imposed rigid shift. I retained that motion—**no rigid alignment or displacement subtraction was applied**.

The **18-replica NEB calculation completed** between the initial and unloaded **zero-strain configurations**, retaining the shear-boundary constraints and continuous periodic x geometry.

## Minimum Energy Path

![NEB energy path between zero-strain configurations](artifacts/analysis/neb/neb-analysis-20261021-215038-5ed9de/neb_mep.png)

The sampled profile contains **one saddle candidate and no intermediate local minima**: energy rises to the saddle and then falls toward the unloaded configuration.

| Quantity | Result |
|---|---:|
| Saddle reaction coordinate | **0.5781** |
| Forward barrier: initial → unloaded | **0.08515 eV** |
| Reverse barrier: unloaded → initial | **0.10108 eV** |
| Unloaded minus initial energy | **−0.01594 eV** |

These energies refer to the **entire simulated cell**, not individual atoms.

### Interpretation

The calculated path is consistent with **two distinct configurations connected by an energy barrier**, with the unloaded state slightly lower in energy. It therefore provides a plausible energetic transition between the states observed before and after loading.

However:

- **NEB force convergence remains unverified** from the available output, so these are **sampled-profile barrier estimates**, not yet validated activation energies.
- The finite replica spacing and sharp decrease after the saddle limit its precise localization.
- This constrained path does **not reconstruct the actual shear-driven trajectory** or establish a unique atomic mechanism.

The most informative next inspection would be the **replica structures before, at, and after the saddle**, to identify which collective motions and local GB rearrangements produce the energy change.

**NEB run:** `neb-20261021-214036-574393`

## 0016 · User

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T22:04:22+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Message

This energy barrier also explains why the initial minimization results in a grain boundary with a relatively high energy. At the same time, I’m wondering what these load drop points have in common. Could you perform a zero-load energy minimization on the configurations corresponding to the points where stress drops during reloading (including the initial point with zero strain)? Do the results obtained after these minimizations have the same final energy?

---

## 0017 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T22:04:22+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

**Yes—all 18 configurations relaxed to the same final energy within numerical resolution.**

I minimized the **reload-start zero-strain configuration** and the **post-drop valleys of all 17 reloading events**. Here, “drop points” means the confirmed valley after each drop, rather than the preceding peak.

### Relaxation conditions

- **No applied loading; all atoms free**, including the former shear-boundary atoms.
- Each simulation box held fixed, with continuous periodic x preserved.
- Same **NiCr.adp** potential, **11,052 atoms**, and identical box for every configuration.
- **All 18 minimizations converged at the force tolerance of \(10^{-6}\) eV/Å**, without warnings.

### Final energies

All rows give **−48,992.820613920 eV** when rounded to nine decimal places. The tiny differences below are relative to the relaxed zero-strain reference.

| Source local step | Historical shear strain | ΔE from relaxed zero-strain reference (eV, approximate) |
|---:|---:|---:|
| **70 — reload start** | **0** | **0** |
| 77 | 0.00175 | −7 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 80 | 0.00250 | −5 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 84 | 0.00350 | −5 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 88 | 0.00450 | −3 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 92 | 0.00550 | −1.6 × 10⁻¹⁰ |
| 96 | 0.00650 | −1.6 × 10⁻¹⁰ |
| 100 | 0.00750 | +1 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 103 | 0.00825 | −8 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 107 | 0.00925 | −3 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 111 | 0.01025 | 0 |
| 115 | 0.01125 | −1.2 × 10⁻¹⁰ |
| 119 | 0.01225 | −9 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 122 | 0.01300 | −1.3 × 10⁻¹⁰ |
| 126 | 0.01400 | −1.6 × 10⁻¹⁰ |
| 130 | 0.01500 | −2 × 10⁻¹¹ |
| 134 | 0.01600 | −1.0 × 10⁻¹⁰ |
| 138 | 0.01700 | −6 × 10⁻¹¹ |

The total energy spread is only **approximately \(1.7\times10^{-10}\) eV for the entire cell**—not a resolved physical difference. Historical strain labels identify the input frames; they are **not final relaxed strains**.

### What this suggests

The reloading valleys share an **energetically equivalent relaxed endpoint** once the displacement constraints are removed. This supports the interpretation that the recurring drops explore equivalent sliding states or states that relax into a common energy basin, rather than progressively accumulating distinct energetic changes.

**Equal energy does not prove identical atomic structures.** A coordinate comparison—allowing for periodic translations and structural equivalence—would distinguish a common configuration from symmetry-equivalent minima.

Also, these are **all-atoms-free energies**. They should not be directly equated with the earlier **shear-constrained NEB energies**. The NEB barrier remains consistent with trapping, but its unverified force convergence prevents treating that explanation as established.

## 0018 · User

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T22:40:39+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Message

This implies that the system returns to the same configuration after each load drop, which is a phenomenon typically associated with a Burgers vector shift. Could you select two consecutive relaxed configurations, output the displacement between these two frames (viewed in plan view), and then determine the relative shift of the two grains？

---

## 0019 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T22:40:39+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

I selected the **first two consecutive relaxed post-drop configurations**, originating from reloading steps **77 → 80**. Both were independently minimized with all atoms free; these are not the original shear-constrained frames.

## Plan view with displacement arrows

![Displacement between consecutive relaxed post-drop configurations](artifacts/analysis/render/render-20261021-224107-c440c3/plan_view.png)

- **View:** XZ, with X right and Z down.
- **Red arrows:** displacement from relaxed step 77 to relaxed step 80, at physical scale without amplification.
- **Black/cream:** bottom/top facing layers.
- All **11,052 atom IDs** were matched. No alignment or common-motion subtraction was applied to these arrows.

The displayed GB atoms have a median displacement magnitude of **0.0955 Å**, with a maximum of **0.3722 Å**.

## Relative shift of the grains

I measured the mean displacement of crystalline interior atoms, excluding **20 Å around the GB and each outer y surface**.

| Grain | Interior atoms | Mean displacement \((\Delta x,\Delta y,\Delta z)\), Å |
|---|---:|---|
| Top — (110) | 3,366 | **(+0.173160, +0.0000096, +0.030477)** |
| Bottom — (111) | 3,348 | **(−0.022514, +0.0000090, −0.030666)** |
| **Top relative to bottom** | — | **(+0.195674, +0.0000006, +0.061143)** |

Thus, the measured relative shift is essentially in the GB plane:

\[
\boxed{\Delta\mathbf{u}_{\mathrm{top-bottom}}
\approx (0.195674\,\hat{x}+0.061143\,\hat{z})\ \text{Å}}
\]

Its **XZ magnitude is 0.205004 Å**. The relative y motion is negligible.

The interiors move almost uniformly: after subtracting each grain’s mean translation **only for a diagnostic**, residual RMS displacements are **0.000741 Å for the top** and **0.000256 Å for the bottom**. Changing the exclusion distance from 10 to 30 Å changes the mean relative-shift vector by less than **10⁻⁷ Å**.

## Is this a Burgers-vector shift?

This comparison demonstrates **collective relative grain translation between equal-energy relaxed states**—they are not atom-by-atom coordinate-identical.

However, the measured **0.205 Å shift does not match a nonzero bulk FCC translation of either grain**. It may represent a boundary-specific sliding increment, but identifying it as a Burgers vector requires additional crystallographic/topological evidence; a DSC-vector analysis was not performed.

Also, these are **two successive post-drop valleys**. Their difference is the net shift between those states, not necessarily the displacement occurring exclusively during one stress drop.

[Detailed shift measurements](artifacts/code/code-20261021-224156-7649e8/published/grain_shift_report.md) · [Numerical data](artifacts/code/code-20261021-224156-7649e8/published/grain_shift.json)  
Sources: `structure-minimize-20261021-220732-c86c47`; displacement render `render-20261021-224107-c440c3`.

### Uncertainty / robustness

No calibrated statistical confidence interval: spatial correlation invalidates naive independent-atom SE. Descriptive independent-atom SE of 20 Å mean relative xyz: (+0.000010861, +0.000007613, +0.000002659).
mean: 10–30 Å component envelope (+0.195673778, +0.000000628, +0.061142946) to (+0.195673791, +0.000000654, +0.061143077); maximum vector difference from 20 Å 9.0273241e-08 Å.

## Collective vs local displacement

| 20 Å grain | raw magnitude median | raw magnitude max | residual RMS | residual median | residual p90 | residual max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| top | 0.175821191 | 0.178880487 | 0.000741485645 | 5.65430004e-05 | 0.00126674033 | 0.00339485056 |
| bottom | 0.038043498 | 0.038771649 | 0.000256112109 | 4.00661461e-05 | 0.000437950233 | 0.00129809037 |

Per-grain mean is subtracted only for these residual diagnostics, not the primary displacement. The top-minus-bottom difference cancels a common translation. Residuals quantify departures from uniform collective motion, without fitting rotations or strain.

## Cubic coefficients / FCC translation test

Top simulation basis: x[001], y[110], z[-1,1,0]; bottom x[1,-2,1], y[111], z[-1,0,1]. Normalized basis columns B convert simulation shift to cubic coordinates as B·shift; a[uvw] coefficients = B·shift/3.52, not integer rounding.

| grain basis | estimator | a[uvw] coefficients | nearest FCC a coefficients | distance Å | nearest nonzero FCC a coefficients | distance Å |
|---|---|---|---|---:|---|---:|
| top | mean | (-0.012282434, +0.012282692, +0.055589142) | (+0.000000000, +0.000000000, +0.000000000) | 0.205004130 | (+0.000000000, +0.500000000, +0.500000000) | 2.322986767 |
| top | median | (-0.012281979, +0.012283177, +0.055589210) | (+0.000000000, +0.000000000, +0.000000000) | 0.205004380 | (+0.000000000, +0.500000000, +0.500000000) | 2.322985315 |
| bottom | mean | (+0.010411715, -0.045388239, +0.034976841) | (+0.000000000, +0.000000000, +0.000000000) | 0.205004130 | (+0.000000000, -0.500000000, +0.500000000) | 2.289425956 |
| bottom | median | (+0.010412111, -0.045387911, +0.034977267) | (+0.000000000, +0.000000000, +0.000000000) | 0.205004380 | (+0.000000000, -0.500000000, +0.500000000) | 2.289425714 |

FCC rule: (a/2)[hkl] with integer h,k,l and even h+k+l, including zero. Both zero and nearest nonzero comparisons are reported; small measured shifts must not be mistaken for a nonzero lattice vector. This is not a DSC test or evidence of a dislocation.

## Independent magnitude reproduction

all: N=11052; magnitude median 0.134172041148, max 0.372204971821 Å.
slab: N=174; magnitude median 0.095543889530, max 0.372204971821 Å.

Slab uses current Y in [-2.0625117292300166, 0.660954760508097] Å to match the existing render. Full magnitude and slab median/max match stored render evidence to &lt;1e-8 Å.

## Limitations

- SE assumes independent atoms and is descriptive only, not a confidence interval: atomic displacements are spatially correlated. Mask sensitivity is the principal robustness check.
- Geometric side assignment plus FCC is not independent crystallographic orientation indexing. Orientations are supplied task metadata.
- MIC cannot infer trajectories or motion exceeding half the box.
- Relative shift need not be a translation of either crystal or a DSC vector; no dislocation/Burgers-vector inference.
- CNA filters local defects; residual statistics describe selected interior atoms only.

## 0020 · User

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T23:15:15+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Message

I noticed something interesting: this translation seems to exhibit a certain mirror symmetry along the tilt axis. Specifically, between two consecutive load drops, the atoms appear to move by some displacement in x direction, accompanied by some displacement in the z direction (i.e., along the tilt axis). Also, the atomic configurations before and after this motion might be mirror images of each other with respect to the z direction. If this is the case, then after two consecutive load drops, applying the mirror operation twice should restore the original atomic configuration. Could you check whether the net displacement between two frames separated by one load drop corresponds to a pure slidng displacement? If so, could you help me determine how many <112> directions this displacement corresponds to? As I recall, the x-direction of the bottom grain is a <112> direction, right?

---

## 0021 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T23:15:15+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

**Yes—the net relative grain motion over two successive increments is nearly pure x sliding.** The alternating z displacement is supported, but **exact mirror equivalence is not established**.

I compared the relaxed post-drop frames **77 → 80 → 84**, so frames 77 and 84 have one intervening post-drop configuration.

### Alternating relative displacement

The vectors below are **top-grain displacement minus bottom-grain displacement**, measured from crystalline interiors without aligning the frames.

| Frame pair | Relative Δx (Å) | Relative Δy (Å) | Relative Δz (Å) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 77 → 80 | +0.195673781 | +0.000000643 | **+0.061142988** |
| 80 → 84 | +0.195674685 | +0.000000038 | **−0.061143214** |
| **77 → 84, direct measurement** | **+0.391348465** | **+0.000000682** | **−0.000000226** |

The z increments cancel almost exactly. The net transverse \((y,z)\) magnitude is only **7.18 × 10⁻⁷ Å**, versus **0.39135 Å along x**. Mean and median estimates agree closely, and changing the interior exclusion distance from 10 to 30 Å has negligible effect.

Thus, **the far-field relative motion is effectively pure sliding along x**. This does not mean every GB atom undergoes precisely the same translation; local structural residuals remain.

### How much of the bottom-grain ⟨112⟩ direction?

You remember correctly: the bottom grain’s signed x direction is

\[
[1\,\overline{2}\,1]\in\langle112\rangle.
\]

Using \(a=3.52\) Å, the net relative displacement is approximately

\[
\boxed{\Delta\mathbf u
=0.04538845\,a[1\,\overline{2}\,1]}
\]

with the tiny transverse residuals shown above retained.

Equivalently,

\[
\boxed{\Delta\mathbf u
=0.09077690\,\frac{a}{2}[1\,\overline{2}\,1]}.
\]

These are **fractions of specified vectors**, not a count of “directions.” The net shift is therefore not one full bulk-lattice translation or an established Burgers vector.

### Does this prove the mirror hypothesis?

Not yet. A separate geometric test reflected the configurations using **\(z\rightarrow -z\) about \(z=0\)**, allowed an x translation, and matched atoms one-to-one while preserving species and grain side.

That test retained an RMS positional mismatch of **0.6184 Å**. By comparison, the unreflected 77 → 84 configurations, after accounting for their measured grain translations, had an RMS mismatch of **0.0203 Å**.

So the evidence supports **alternating z motion followed by near-pure x translation**, rather than exact mirror equivalence about the tested plane. A differently located reflection plane remains untested. Applying a reflection twice would be the identity mathematically, but that alone does not establish that the observed increments are reflections.

<pre>{&quot;status&quot;: &quot;ok&quot;, &quot;summary&quot;: &quot;The alternating relative-Z hypothesis is supported, with nearly pure-X net motion from frame 77 to 84: mean X=0.391348465 Å and transverse norm=7.18×10⁻⁷ Å across 11,052 matched IDs. Net coefficients are c=0.04538845 for full a[1,−2,1] and 2c=0.09077690 for the half-vector. Masks, sensitivity, additivity and residuals were published. Validation passed, but independent scientific checks were not applied; mirror interpretation remains limited by the fixed z₀=0 origin.&quot;, &quot;data&quot;: {&quot;run&quot;: {&quot;run_id&quot;: &quot;code-20261021-231633-f084af&quot;, &quot;workflow&quot;: &quot;code&quot;, &quot;status&quot;: &quot;completed&quot;, &quot;manifest&quot;: &quot;artifacts/code/code-20261021-231633-f084af/manifest.json&quot;, &quot;full_output&quot;: {&quot;tool&quot;: &quot;query_project_memory&quot;, &quot;entity&quot;: &quot;runs&quot;, &quot;run_id&quot;: &quot;code-20261021-231633-f084af&quot;}, &quot;artifact_roles&quot;: [&quot;config&quot;, &quot;measurement_script&quot;, &quot;memory_facts&quot;, &quot;source_input&quot;, &quot;two_drop_report&quot;, &quot;two_drop_shift_data&quot;], &quot;conclusion_lookup_key&quot;: &quot;run:code-20261021-231633-f084af:main_conclusion&quot;, &quot;result&quot;: {&quot;summary&quot;: &quot;Exact 11052-ID three-frame MIC translations confirm alternating relative Z and nearly pure-X net 77→84. ### Geometric strain fit

- Original misorientation: **35.264390°**, not snapped to CSL

This is a **homogeneous geometric fit**, not an energy minimization or a modification of your simulated GB.

## Geometric displacement candidates

Both layers **A and B** were searched. Every edge minimum occurs in **layer A**, with two tied, opposite vectors.

The notations below represent the **same spatial displacement in the two strained grain bases**, with lattice factor \(a_0=3.52\) Å. The ± signs are correlated between columns.

| Cell edges | Shortest nonzero distance | Layer | G1 representation | G2 representation |
|---|---:|:---:|---|---|
| C1–C2 and C3–C4 | **0.3915131 Å** | A | \(\pm\frac{a_0}{9}[0,0,1]\) | \(\pm\frac{a_0}{22}[1,1,2]\) |
| C2–C3 and C4–C1 | **0.2768415 Å** | A | \(\pm\frac{a_0}{22}[1,1,1]\) | \(\pm\frac{a_0}{18}[1,1,0]\) |

Thus, **0.2768415 Å is the smallest nonzero edge candidate overall**; the **0.3915131 Å** candidate is the one closest in length to your measured **0.391348465 Å** two-drop slide.

That length agreement is promising, but **direction agreement still needs checking**: the map’s G2 indices are in its own lattice basis and must not be directly equated with the simulation’s bottom-local \([1,\overline2,1]\). These remain **geometric Burgers-vector candidates**, not confirmed DSC vectors or identified dislocations.

## 0029 · Assistant

> status: `completed` · created: `2026-10-21T23:39:55+00:00` · thread: `c1d2f74f-95b9-4aba-a105-126d2a65d6ac`

### Answer

## Project summary

We studied a **general asymmetric tilt GB in FCC Ni**, with one side in **{110}**, the other in **{111}**, and a physical tilt axis in **⟨110⟩**. The work progressed from crystallographic screening to relaxation, loading–unloading, transition-path analysis, and dichromatic-map exploration.

On your latest point: **[112] and [1,−2,1] belong to the same cubic ⟨112⟩ family**, so they are symmetry-equivalent direction types. This strengthens the correspondence between the dichromatic candidate and the measured sliding increment. However, family equivalence alone does not establish equality of the **physical vectors in the fixed bicrystal frame**; that requires consistently mapping both grain bases and the boundary geometry.

## 1. Crystallography and initial structure

We first screened FCC ⟨110⟩ CSL boundaries with **Σ ≤ 50**, then sought low-index approximations with **≤5° deviation per plane and ≤5° misorientation change**.

- The bounded source enumeration contained nine nontrivial Σ values and ten rotation branches.
- Screening the returned low-index-side pairs gave **15 near-CSL candidates and two unchanged exact pairs**.
- The bounded plane search was not a certified exhaustive enumeration.

For the selected **{110} ∥ {111}** boundary, the family constraints resolved to one physically distinct general-tilt geometry:

| Grain | GB plane | Local x | Local tilt direction z |
|---|---|---|---|
| Top | (110) | [001] | [−1,1,0] |
| Bottom | (111) | [1,−2,1] | [−1,0,1] |

The misorientation is **35.264390°**, with no exact Σ assigned. The denser **(111)** side was placed at the bottom.

The initial vacuum-separated-x structure used **NiCr.adp**, contained **56,880 atoms**, and had top/bottom repeats **[57,21,3] / [23,17,3]**. Its construction mismatch was **1.16094%**.

**Evidence:** `csl-20261021-201945-0edf88`; `minimize-20261021-202104-4198e0`.  
**Qualification:** the initial minimization completed but did not meet its final force tolerance.

## 2. Structural-period analysis and the P012 cell

Joint coordinate, atomic-row, and elastic-strain analysis identified **P012** as the strongest candidate for review:

- Coordinate recurrence: **3.874375 nm**
- Associated atomic-row motif: **3.880349 nm**
- Nominal translations: **11a[001] / 4.5a[1,−2,1]**
- Construction mismatch: **0.205973%**

These nearby lengths were supporting, complementary observations—not assumed identical measurements.

You selected P012, and we built a **continuous-periodic-x** structure:

| Grain | Repeats | Nominal material dimensions |
|---|---|---|
| Top | [11,21,3] | 3.872 × 10.454 × 1.493 nm |
| Bottom | [4.5,17,3] | 3.880 × 10.365 × 1.493 nm |

The cell contains **11,052 atoms**. The fractional bottom repeat was preserved exactly.

**Evidence:** `gb-periodicity-20261021-202310-f3831c`; `minimize-20261021-202820-718597`.  
**Qualification:** the P012 minimization’s final stage reached its iteration limit rather than force tolerance; nominal lattice closure did not independently validate the relaxed periodic seam.

## 3. Shear loading and the history-dependent response

We applied **70 +x displacement increments of 2.5 × 10⁻⁴**, reaching **1.75% shear strain**, then continued with **70 unloading increments to zero** and **70 reloading increments to the original displacement**.

| Result | First loading | Reloading |
|---|---:|---:|
| Largest confirmed interior stress-magnitude peak | **788.657 MPa** | **20.956 MPa** |
| Largest peak-to-valley magnitude drop | **786.146 MPa** | **19.580 MPa** |
| Strain interval of largest drop | 0.00850 → 0.00925 | 0.01550 → 0.01600 |

**The original large collapse did not recur:** the largest reload drop was about **40 times smaller**. Subsequent loading showed recurring, much smaller stress fluctuations.

At zero imposed strain after unloading:

- Residual top shear stress: **12.105 MPa**
- Median atomic displacement from the initial zero-strain frame: **0.2971 Å**
- Maximum displacement: **0.7805 Å**

Thus, restoring the imposed strain did not restore the initial atomic coordinates.

**Evidence:** `shear-20261021-203147-9d18c4`; `shear-20261021-211934-d84742`; `render-20261021-213748-f27cfe`.  
**Qualification:** all first-loading points converged; two continuation points did not and were retained in the analysis.

## 4. NEB and independently relaxed post-drop states

### Zero-strain transition path

An **18-replica, shear-constrained NEB** connected the initial and unloaded zero-strain configurations without removing their emergent collective motion.

| Sampled-profile quantity | Result |
|---|---:|
| Saddle candidates | **1** |
| Forward barrier | **0.08515 eV** |
| Reverse barrier | **0.10108 eV** |
| Final minus initial energy | **−0.01594 eV** |

These are **whole-cell energies**. The profile is consistent with a barrier separating the two states, but **NEB force convergence was not verified**, so the barrier values remain provisional.

**Evidence:** `neb-20261021-214036-574393`; `neb-analysis-20261021-215038-ae08e5`.

### Zero-load relaxation of reloading valleys

We independently minimized the reload-start configuration and **all 17 confirmed post-drop valleys**, with:

- All atoms free
- No applied loads
- Identical fixed boxes and potential
- Force tolerance **10⁻⁶ eV/Å**

**All 18 converged**, reaching approximately:

\[
E_{\mathrm{final}}=-48\,992.820613920\ \mathrm{eV}.
\]

The entire energy spread was only **1.7 × 10⁻¹⁰ eV**, with no resolved physical energy difference.

This establishes **energetically equivalent relaxed states**, not necessarily identical coordinates. These all-free energies are also not directly equivalent to the shear-constrained NEB energies.

**Evidence:** `structure-minimize-20261021-220732-c86c47`.

## 5. Sliding increments and dichromatic corroboration

For consecutive relaxed valleys **77 → 80 → 84**, the far-field top-minus-bottom shifts were:

| Pair | Δx | Δz |
|---|---:|---:|
| 77 → 80 | +0.195674 Å | +0.061143 Å |
| 80 → 84 | +0.195675 Å | −0.061143 Å |
| **77 → 84** | **+0.391348 Å** | **−0.000000226 Å** |

The net transverse displacement was only **7.18 × 10⁻⁷ Å**. Therefore, the two-increment net relative motion is effectively **pure x sliding**:

\[
\Delta\mathbf u
\approx 0.04538845\,a[1,\overline2,1]
\approx \frac{a}{22}[1,\overline2,1].
\]

The rational approximation differs from the measured length by about **0.15%**.

The z components alternate, but an exact mirror relationship about the tested **z = 0** plane was not established.

After you selected a dichromatic cell, its geometric fit required only **0.126448% maximum principal strain**. Both FCC axial layers were analyzed, giving:

| Geometric candidate | Minimum length | Layer |
|---|---:|---|
| \(a_0[001]/9\) in G1; \(a_0[112]/22\) in G2 | **0.391513 Å** | A |
| \(a_0[111]/22\) in G1; \(a_0[110]/18\) in G2 | **0.276842 Å** | A |

The **0.391513 Å** candidate closely matches the measured **0.391348 Å** slide and has the corresponding **⟨112⟩ family character** in G2. This is useful geometric corroboration, although the map describes a slightly strained geometry.

**Evidence:** `code-20261021-231633-f084af`; `dichromatic-map-20261021-233535-774f02`.

## Brief conclusions

1. **The GB response is strongly history-dependent.** The first loading produces a large stress collapse that does not recover after unloading and reloading to the same displacement.

2. **The reloading valleys relax to energetically equivalent states.** All 18 independent, all-free minimizations converged to the same energy within numerical resolution.

3. **Recurring sliding has an alternating transverse component.** Successive z shifts cancel, leaving a robust two-increment x shift of approximately **\(a[1,\overline2,1]/22\)**.

4. **The dichromatic analysis supports a boundary-specific geometric shift candidate.** Its **0.391513 Å, ⟨112⟩-family** vector closely corresponds to the measured increment. It is a strong candidate for further Burgers-vector analysis, **not yet a confirmed Burgers vector or identified dislocation**.

5. **The transition-path evidence is suggestive rather than final.** The sampled NEB profile contains a barrier, but convergence and intermediate-structure inspection are still needed before assigning a validated activation barrier or unique mechanism.
